href="/wiki/Lutheranism" title="Lutheranism">Lutheranism</a><br /><a href="/wiki/Islam" title="Islam">Islam</a><br /><a href="/wiki/Afro-American_religion" class="mw-redirect" title="Afro-American religion">Afro-American religion</a></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label"><a href="/wiki/Canonization" title="Canonization">Canonized</a></th><td class="infobox-data"><a href="/wiki/Pre-Congregation" class="mw-redirect" title="Pre-Congregation">Pre-Congregation</a></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label"><span class="nowrap">Major <a href="/wiki/Shrine" title="Shrine">shrine</a></span></th><td class="infobox-data"><a href="/wiki/Apt_Cathedral" title="Apt Cathedral">Apt Cathedral</a>, <a href="/wiki/Sainte-Anne-d%27Auray" title="Sainte-Anne-d&#39;Auray">Basilica of Sainte-Anne d'Auray</a>, <a href="/wiki/Basilica_of_Sainte-Anne-de-Beaupr%C3%A9" title="Basilica of Sainte-Anne-de-Beaupré">Basilica of Sainte-Anne-de-Beaupré</a></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label"><a href="/wiki/Calendar_of_saints" title="Calendar of saints">Feast</a></th><td class="infobox-data">26 July (<a href="/wiki/Roman_Catholic" class="mw-redirect" title="Roman Catholic">Roman Catholic</a>),<sup id="cite_ref-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-1">&#91;1&#93;</a></sup><br /> 
9 September (<a href="/wiki/Eastern_Orthodox" class="mw-redirect" title="Eastern Orthodox">Eastern Orthodox</a>)<sup id="cite_ref-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-2">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label"><a href="/wiki/Saint_symbolism" title="Saint symbolism">Attributes</a></th><td class="infobox-data">Book; door; with Mary, Jesus or Joachim; woman dressed in red or green<sup id="cite_ref-Fongemie_3-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Fongemie-3">&#91;3&#93;</a></sup></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label"><a href="/wiki/Patron_saint" title="Patron saint">Patronage</a></th><td class="infobox-data">Mothers, grandparents, pregnant women, children, unmarried people, teachers, carpenters, child care providers, seamstresses, lacemakers, secondhand-clothes dealers, equestrians, stablemen, miners, lost things, moving homes, poverty, sterility, <a href="/wiki/Brittany" title="Brittany">Brittany</a>, <a href="/wiki/Canada" title="Canada">Canada</a>, <a href="/wiki/Detroit" title="Detroit">Detroit</a>, <a href="/wiki/Taguig" title="Taguig">Taguig</a>, <a href="/wiki/Triana,_Seville" title="Triana, Seville">Triana, Seville</a>, <a href="/wiki/Hagonoy,_Bulacan" title="Hagonoy, Bulacan">Hagonoy, Bulacan</a>, <a href="/wiki/Barili,_Cebu" class="mw-redirect" title="Barili, Cebu">Barili, Cebu</a>, <a href="/wiki/Molo,_Iloilo_City" title="Molo, Iloilo City">Molo, Iloilo City</a>, <a href="/wiki/Roman_Catholic_Diocese_of_Kurunegala" title="Roman Catholic Diocese of Kurunegala">Kurunegala Catholic Diocese, Sri Lanka</a>, <a href="/wiki/Fasnia" title="Fasnia">Fasnia, Tenerife</a>, <a href="/wiki/Mainar" title="Mainar">Mainar</a>, <a href="/wiki/Marsaskala" title="Marsaskala">Marsaskala</a>, <a href="/wiki/Carmelites" title="Carmelites">Carmelites</a></td></tr></tbody></table>
<p>According to <a href="/wiki/Christianity" title="Christianity">Christian</a> <a href="/wiki/Apocrypha" title="Apocrypha">apocryphal</a> and <a href="/wiki/Islam" title="Islam">Islamic</a> tradition, <b>Saint Anne</b> was the mother of <a href="/wiki/Mary,_mother_of_Jesus" title="Mary, mother of Jesus">Mary</a>, the wife of <a href="/wiki/Joachim" title="Joachim">Joachim</a> and the maternal grandmother of <a href="/wiki/Jesus" title="Jesus">Jesus</a>. Mary's mother is not named in the <a href="/wiki/Gospel#Canonical_gospels" title="Gospel">canonical gospels</a>. In writing, Anne's name and that of her husband <a href="/wiki/Joachim" title="Joachim">Joachim</a> come only from <a href="/wiki/New_Testament_apocrypha" title="New Testament apocrypha">New Testament apocrypha</a>, of which the <a href="/wiki/Gospel_of_James" title="Gospel of James">Gospel of James</a> (written perhaps around 150 AD) seems to be the earliest that mentions them.
The mother of Mary is mentioned but not named in the <a href="/wiki/Quran" title="Quran">Quran</a>.
</p>
<meta property="mw:PageProp/toc" />
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Christian_tradition">Christian tradition</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Saint_Anne&amp;action=edit&amp;section=1" title="Edit section: Christian tradition">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>The story is similar to that of <a href="/wiki/Samuel" title="Samuel">Samuel</a>, whose mother <a href="/wiki/Hannah_(biblical_figure)" title="Hannah (biblical figure)">Hannah</a> (<a href="/wiki/Hebrew_language" title="Hebrew language">Hebrew</a>: <span lang="he" dir="rtl"><style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1020748213">.mw-parser-output .script-hebrew,.mw-parser-output .script-Hebr{font-family:"SBL Hebrew","SBL BibLit","Taamey Ashkenaz","Taamey Frank CLM","Frank Ruehl CLM","Ezra SIL","Ezra SIL SR","Keter Aram Tsova","Taamey David CLM","Keter YG","Shofar","David CLM","Hadasim CLM","Simple CLM","Nachlieli",Cardo,Alef,"Noto Serif Hebrew","Noto Sans Hebrew","David Libre",David,"Times New Roman",Gisha,Arial,FreeSerif,FreeSans}</style><span class="script-hebrew" style="font-size: 115%;" dir="rtl">חַנָּה</span>&#8206;</span> <i>Ḥannāh</i> "favour, grace"; etymologically the same name as Anne) had also been childless. The <a href="/wiki/Immaculate_Conception" title="Immaculate Conception">Immaculate Conception</a> was eventually made dogma by the <a href="/wiki/Catholic_Church" title="Catholic Church">Catholic Church</a> following an increased devotion to Anne in the twelfth century.<sup id="cite_ref-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-4">&#91;4&#93;</a></sup> Dedications to Anne in <a href="/wiki/Eastern_Christianity" title="Eastern Christianity">Eastern Christianity</a> occur as early as the sixth century.<sup id="cite_ref-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-5">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup> In the <a href="/wiki/Eastern_Orthodox_Church" title="Eastern Orthodox Church">Eastern Orthodox tradition</a>, Anne and <a href="/wiki/Joachim" title="Joachim">Joachim</a> are ascribed the title <i>Ancestors of God</i>,<sup id="cite_ref-6" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-6">&#91;6&#93;</a></sup> and both the <a href="/wiki/Nativity_of_Mary" title="Nativity of Mary">Nativity of Mary</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Presentation_of_Mary" title="Presentation of Mary">Presentation of Mary</a> are celebrated as two of the twelve <a href="/wiki/Great_Feasts_of_the_Orthodox_Church" class="mw-redirect" title="Great Feasts of the Orthodox Church">Great Feasts of the Orthodox Church</a>. The <a href="https://en.wiktionary.org/wiki/Dormition" class="extiw" title="wikt:Dormition">Dormition of Anne</a> is also a minor feast in Eastern Christianity. In <a href="/wiki/Lutheran" class="mw-redirect" title="Lutheran">Lutheran</a> <a href="/wiki/Protestantism" title="Protestantism">Protestantism</a>, it is held that <a href="/wiki/Martin_Luther" title="Martin Luther">Martin Luther</a> chose to enter religious life as an <a href="/wiki/Augustinians" title="Augustinians">Augustinian friar</a> after invoking St. Anne while endangered by lightning.<sup id="cite_ref-Brecht1985_7-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Brecht1985-7">&#91;7&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="In_Islam">In Islam</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Saint_Anne&amp;action=edit&amp;section=2" title="Edit section: In Islam">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>Anne (<a href="/wiki/Arabic_language" class="mw-redirect" title="Arabic language">Arabic</a>: <span lang="ar" dir="rtl">حنة</span>, <small><a href="/wiki/Romanization_of_Arabic" title="Romanization of Arabic">romanized</a>:&#160;</small><span title="Arabic-language romanization"><i lang="ar-Latn"><i>Ḥannah</i></i></span>) is also revered in <a href="/wiki/Islam" title="Islam">Islam</a>, recognized as a highly spiritual woman and as the mother of Mary. <a href="/wiki/Women_in_the_Quran" title="Women in the Quran">She is not named in the Quran</a>, where she is referred to as "the wife of Imran". The Quran describes her remaining childless until her old age. One day, Hannah saw a bird feeding its young while sitting in the shade of a tree, which awakened her desire to have children of her own. She prayed for a child and eventually conceived; her husband, <a href="/wiki/Joachim#In_Islam" title="Joachim">Imran</a>, died before the child was born. Expecting the child to be male, Hannah vowed to dedicate him to isolation and service in the <a href="/wiki/Second_Temple" title="Second Temple">Second Temple</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-8" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-8">&#91;N 1&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Wheeler_9-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Wheeler-9">&#91;8&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-DaCosta_10-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-DaCosta-10">&#91;9&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>
However, Hannah bore a daughter instead, and named her Mary. Her words upon delivering Mary reflect her status as a great <a href="/wiki/Mysticism" title="Mysticism">mystic</a>, realising that while she had wanted a son, this daughter was God's gift to her:<sup id="cite_ref-Wheeler_9-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Wheeler-9">&#91;8&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-DaCosta_10-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-DaCosta-10">&#91;9&#93;</a></sup><style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r996844942">.mw-parser-output .templatequote{overflow:hidden;margin:1em 0;padding:0 40px}.mw-parser-output .templatequote .templatequotecite{line-height:1.5em;text-align:left;padding-left:1.6em;margin-top:0}</style></p><blockquote class="templatequote"><p>When she delivered, she said, “My Lord! I have given birth to a girl,”—and Allah fully knew what she had delivered—“and the male is not like the female. I have named her Mary, and I seek Your protection for her and her offspring from Satan, the accursed.” So her Lord accepted her graciously and blessed her with a pleasant upbringing—entrusting her to the care of Zachariah...</p><div class="templatequotecite">—&#8202;<cite><a href="/wiki/Al_Imran" title="Al Imran">Surah Al Imran</a> <a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://quran.com/3?startingVerse=36">3:36-37</a></cite></div></blockquote>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Beliefs">Beliefs</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Saint_Anne&amp;action=edit&amp;section=3" title="Edit section: Beliefs">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Saint_Anne_et_Marie_enfant.JPG" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f5/Saint_Anne_et_Marie_enfant.JPG/220px-Saint_Anne_et_Marie_enfant.JPG" decoding="async" width="220" height="293" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f5/Saint_Anne_et_Marie_enfant.JPG/330px-Saint_Anne_et_Marie_enfant.JPG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f5/Saint_Anne_et_Marie_enfant.JPG/440px-Saint_Anne_et_Marie_enfant.JPG 2x" data-file-width="3240" data-file-height="4320" /></a><figcaption>Saint Anne with Mary as a child</figcaption></figure>
<p>Although the <a href="/wiki/Biblical_canon#Christian_canons" title="Biblical canon">canonical books of the New Testament</a> never mention the mother of the Virgin Mary, traditions about her family, childhood, education, and eventual betrothal to Joseph developed very early in the history of the church. The oldest and most influential source for these is the apocryphal <a href="/wiki/Gospel_of_James" title="Gospel of James">Gospel of James</a>, first written in <a href="/wiki/Koine_Greek" title="Koine Greek">Koine Greek</a> around the middle of the second century AD. In the West, the Gospel of James fell under a cloud in the fourth and fifth centuries when it was accused of "absurdities" by <a href="/wiki/Jerome" title="Jerome">Jerome</a> and condemned as untrustworthy by <a href="/wiki/Pope_Damasus_I" title="Pope Damasus I">Pope Damasus I</a>, <a href="/wiki/Pope_Innocent_I" title="Pope Innocent I">Pope Innocent I</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Pope_Gelasius_I" title="Pope Gelasius I">Pope Gelasius I</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-reames_11-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-reames-11">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup> However, despite having been condemned by the Church, it was taken over almost <i>in toto</i> by another apocryphal work, the <a href="/wiki/Gospel_of_Pseudo-Matthew" title="Gospel of Pseudo-Matthew">Gospel of Pseudo-Matthew</a>, which popularised most of its stories.<sup id="cite_ref-12" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-12">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Ancient belief, attested to by a sermon of <a href="/wiki/John_of_Damascus" title="John of Damascus">John of Damascus</a>, was that Anne married once. In the <a href="/wiki/Late_Middle_Ages" title="Late Middle Ages">Late Middle Ages</a>, legend held that Anne was married three times: first to Joachim, then to <a href="/wiki/Clopas" title="Clopas">Clopas</a> and finally to a man named Solomas and that each marriage produced one daughter: Mary, mother of Jesus, <a href="/wiki/Mary_of_Clopas" title="Mary of Clopas">Mary of Clopas</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Salome_(disciple)" title="Salome (disciple)">Mary Salome</a>, respectively.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (September 2022)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> The sister of Saint Anne was <a href="/wiki/Sobe_(sister_of_Saint_Anne)" title="Sobe (sister of Saint Anne)">Sobe</a>, mother of <a href="/wiki/Elizabeth_(biblical_figure)" title="Elizabeth (biblical figure)">Elizabeth</a>. In the fifteenth century, the Catholic cleric <a href="/wiki/Johann_Eck" title="Johann Eck">Johann Eck</a> related in a sermon that St Anne's parents were named Stollanus and <a href="/wiki/Emerentia" title="Emerentia">Emerentia</a>. <a href="/wiki/Frederick_George_Holweck" title="Frederick George Holweck">Frederick George Holweck</a>, writing in the <i><a href="/wiki/Catholic_Encyclopedia" title="Catholic Encyclopedia">Catholic Encyclopedia</a></i> (1907) regards this genealogy as spurious.<sup id="cite_ref-holweck_13-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-holweck-13">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In the fourth century and then much later in the fifteenth century, a belief arose that Mary was conceived of Anne without <a href="/wiki/Original_sin" title="Original sin">original sin</a>. This belief in the <a href="/wiki/Immaculate_Conception" title="Immaculate Conception">Immaculate Conception</a> states that God preserved Mary's body and soul intact and sinless from her first moment of existence, through the merits of Jesus Christ.<sup id="cite_ref-holweck_13-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-holweck-13">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup> The Immaculate Conception, often confused with the <a href="/wiki/Annunciation" title="Annunciation">Annunciation</a> of the <a href="/wiki/Incarnation_(Christianity)" title="Incarnation (Christianity)">Incarnation</a> (Mary's virgin birth of Jesus), was made <a href="/wiki/Dogma_in_the_Catholic_Church" title="Dogma in the Catholic Church">dogma</a> in the Catholic church by <a href="/wiki/Pope_Pius_IX" title="Pope Pius IX">Pope Pius IX</a>'s <a href="/wiki/Papal_bull" title="Papal bull">papal bull</a>, <a href="/wiki/Ineffabilis_Deus" title="Ineffabilis Deus"><i>Ineffabilis</i> <i>Deus</i></a>, in 1854.
</p><p>The thirteenth century <i><a href="/wiki/Speculum_Maius" title="Speculum Maius">Speculum Maius</a></i> of <a href="/wiki/Vincent_of_Beauvais" title="Vincent of Beauvais">Vincent of Beauvais</a> incorporates information regarding the life of Saint Anne from an earlier work by <a href="/wiki/Hrotsvitha" title="Hrotsvitha">Hrotsvitha</a> of Gandersheim Abbey.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTENixon200412_14-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTENixon200412-14">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup> 
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Veneration">Veneration</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Saint_Anne&amp;action=edit&amp;section=4" title="Edit section: Veneration">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Adriaen_van_Overbeke_-_Birth_of_St._Anne_-_Petrikirche_altarpiece.jpeg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d8/Adriaen_van_Overbeke_-_Birth_of_St._Anne_-_Petrikirche_altarpiece.jpeg/220px-Adriaen_van_Overbeke_-_Birth_of_St._Anne_-_Petrikirche_altarpiece.jpeg" decoding="async" width="220" height="222" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d8/Adriaen_van_Overbeke_-_Birth_of_St._Anne_-_Petrikirche_altarpiece.jpeg/330px-Adriaen_van_Overbeke_-_Birth_of_St._Anne_-_Petrikirche_altarpiece.jpeg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d8/Adriaen_van_Overbeke_-_Birth_of_St._Anne_-_Petrikirche_altarpiece.jpeg/440px-Adriaen_van_Overbeke_-_Birth_of_St._Anne_-_Petrikirche_altarpiece.jpeg 2x" data-file-width="519" data-file-height="524" /></a><figcaption><i>Birth of St. Anne</i>, by <a href="/wiki/Adriaen_van_Overbeke" title="Adriaen van Overbeke">Adriaen van Overbeke</a> (<abbr title="circa">c.</abbr><span style="white-space:nowrap;">&#8201;1521</span>–1525)</figcaption></figure>
<p>In the Eastern church, the <a href="/wiki/Veneration#Christianity" title="Veneration">veneration</a> of Anne herself may go back as far as <abbr title="circa">c.</abbr><span style="white-space:nowrap;">&#8201;550</span>, when Justinian built a church in Constantinople in her honor.<sup id="cite_ref-15" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-15">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup> The earliest pictorial sign of her veneration in the West is an eighth-century fresco in the church of <a href="/wiki/Santa_Maria_Antiqua" title="Santa Maria Antiqua">Santa Maria Antiqua</a>, Rome.<sup id="cite_ref-reames_11-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-reames-11">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Feast_of_the_Conception_of_the_Virgin_Mary" title="Feast of the Conception of the Virgin Mary">Feast of the Conception of the Virgin Mary</a> had reached southern Italy by the ninth century. The cult of Saint Anne had developed in northern Europe by the twelfth century. A shrine at Douai, in northern France, was one of the early centers of devotion to St. Anne in the West.<sup id="cite_ref-crawley_16-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-crawley-16">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Virgin_and_Child_with_Saint_Anne" title="Virgin and Child with Saint Anne"><i>Anna Selbdritt</i></a> was a type of iconography depicting the 3 generations of Saint Anne, Mary, and the child Jesus. Emphasizing the humanity of Jesus, it drew on the earlier conventions of the <a href="/wiki/Seat_of_Wisdom" title="Seat of Wisdom">Seat of Wisdom</a>, and was popular in northern Germany in the 1500s.<sup id="cite_ref-17" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-17">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>During the High Middle Ages, Saint Anne became increasingly identified as a maritime saint, protecting sailors and fisherman, and invoked against storms.<sup id="cite_ref-ottawa_18-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ottawa-18">&#91;17&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Two well-known shrines to St. Anne are that of <a href="/wiki/Sainte-Anne-d%27Auray" title="Sainte-Anne-d&#39;Auray">Ste-Anne-d'Auray</a> in Brittany, France; and that of <a href="/wiki/Basilica_of_Sainte-Anne-de-Beaupr%C3%A9" title="Basilica of Sainte-Anne-de-Beaupré">Ste-Anne-de-Beaupré</a> near the city of Québec. The number of visitors to the Basilica of Ste-Anne-de-Beaupré is greatest on St Anne's Feast Day, 26 July, and the Sunday before Nativity of the Virgin Mary, 8 September. In 1892, Pope Leo XIII sent a relic of St Anne to the church.<sup id="cite_ref-ottawa_18-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ottawa-18">&#91;17&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In the <a href="/wiki/Maltese_language" title="Maltese language">Maltese language</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Milky_Way" title="Milky Way">Milky Way</a> <a href="/wiki/Galaxy" title="Galaxy">galaxy</a> is called <i>It-Triq ta' Sant'Anna</i>, literally <i>"The Way of St. Anne"</i>.<sup id="cite_ref-malt_TheM_19-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-malt_TheM-19">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In Imperial Russia, the <a href="/wiki/Order_of_St_Anna" class="mw-redirect" title="Order of St Anna">Order of St Anne</a> was one of the leading state decorations.
</p><p>In the United States, the <a href="/wiki/Daughters_of_the_Holy_Spirit" title="Daughters of the Holy Spirit">Daughters of the Holy Spirit</a> named the former <a href="/wiki/Annhurst_College" title="Annhurst College">Annhurst College</a> in her honor.<sup id="cite_ref-20" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-20">&#91;19&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Commemoration">Commemoration</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Saint_Anne&amp;action=edit&amp;section=5" title="Edit section: Commemoration">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Feast_of_Saint_Anne_in_Marsaskala_Malta.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c3/Feast_of_Saint_Anne_in_Marsaskala_Malta.jpg/220px-Feast_of_Saint_Anne_in_Marsaskala_Malta.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c3/Feast_of_Saint_Anne_in_Marsaskala_Malta.jpg/330px-Feast_of_Saint_Anne_in_Marsaskala_Malta.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c3/Feast_of_Saint_Anne_in_Marsaskala_Malta.jpg/440px-Feast_of_Saint_Anne_in_Marsaskala_Malta.jpg 2x" data-file-width="4128" data-file-height="3096" /></a><figcaption>Feast of Saint Anne in <a href="/w/index.php?title=Marsaskala_Parish_Church&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Marsaskala Parish Church (page does not exist)">Marsaskala Parish Church</a><span class="noprint" style="font-size:85%; font-style: normal;">&#160;&#91;<a href="https://fr.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C3%89glise_Sainte-Anne_de_Marsaskala" class="extiw" title="fr:Église Sainte-Anne de Marsaskala">fr</a>&#93;</span>, <a href="/wiki/Marsaskala" title="Marsaskala">Marsaskala</a>, Malta</figcaption></figure>
<p>By the middle of the seventh century, a distinct feast day, the Conception of St. Anne (Maternity of Holy Anna) celebrating the conception of Mary by Saint Anne, was observed at the <a href="/wiki/Mar_Saba" title="Mar Saba">Monastery of Saint Sabas</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-21" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-21">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup> It is now known in the Greek Orthodox Church as the feast of "<a href="/wiki/Feast_of_the_Conception_of_the_Virgin_Mary" title="Feast of the Conception of the Virgin Mary">The Conception by St. Anne of the Most Holy Theotokos</a>", and celebrated on 9 December.<sup id="cite_ref-22" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-22">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup> In the Roman Catholic Church, the Feast of Saints Anne and Joachim is celebrated on 26 July.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Feast_Day">Feast Day</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Saint_Anne&amp;action=edit&amp;section=6" title="Edit section: Feast Day">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>

<ul><li>26 July</li></ul>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Eastern_Orthodox_Church">Eastern Orthodox Church</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Saint_Anne&amp;action=edit&amp;section=8" title="Edit section: Eastern Orthodox Church">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>

<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Anglican_Communion">Anglican Communion</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Saint_Anne&amp;action=edit&amp;section=9" title="Edit section: Anglican Communion">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>

<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Lutheranism">Lutheranism</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Saint_Anne&amp;action=edit&amp;section=10" title="Edit section: Lutheranism">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<ul><li>26 July</li></ul>

<h4><span id="Syro-Malabar_Church.5B25.5D"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Syro-Malabar_Church[25]">Syro-Malabar Church<sup id="cite_ref-26" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-26">&#91;25&#93;</a></sup></span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Saint_Anne&amp;action=edit&amp;section=13" title="Edit section: Syro-Malabar Church[25]">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>

<h4><span id="Maronite_Church.5B27.5D"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Maronite_Church[27]">Maronite Church<sup id="cite_ref-28" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-28">&#91;27&#93;</a></sup></span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Saint_Anne&amp;action=edit&amp;section=15" title="Edit section: Maronite Church[27]">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<ul><li>9 September (St. Anne and <a href="/wiki/Joachim" title="Joachim">Joachim</a>, <a href="/wiki/Mary,_mother_of_Jesus" title="Mary, mother of Jesus">Parents of the Blessed Virgin Mary</a>)</li></ul>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Relics">Relics</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Saint_Anne&amp;action=edit&amp;section=16" title="Edit section: Relics">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>The alleged relics of St. Anne were brought from the <a href="/wiki/Holy_Land" title="Holy Land">Holy Land</a> to <a href="/wiki/Constantinople" title="Constantinople">Constantinople</a> in 710 and were kept there in the church of St. Sophia as late as 1333.<sup id="cite_ref-holweck_13-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-holweck-13">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>During the twelfth and thirteenth centuries, returning crusaders and pilgrims from the East brought relics of Anne to a number of churches, including most famously those at Apt, in Provence, Ghent, and Chartres.<sup id="cite_ref-reames_11-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-reames-11">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup> St. Anne's relics have been preserved and venerated in the many cathedrals and monasteries dedicated to her name, for example in <a href="/wiki/St._Anne%27s_Church,_Vienna" title="St. Anne&#39;s Church, Vienna">Austria</a>, Canada,<sup id="cite_ref-29" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-29">&#91;28&#93;</a></sup> Germany, Italy,<sup id="cite_ref-30" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-30">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup> and Greece in the semi-autonomous <a href="/wiki/Mount_Athos" title="Mount Athos">Mount Athos</a>, and the city of Katerini.<sup id="cite_ref-31" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-31">&#91;30&#93;</a></sup> Medieval and baroque craftsmanship is evidenced in, for example, the metalwork of the life-size reliquaries containing the bones of her forearm. Examples employing folk art techniques are also known.
</p><p><a href="/wiki/D%C3%BCren" title="Düren">Düren</a> has been the main place of pilgrimage for Anne since 1506, when <a href="/wiki/Pope_Julius_II" title="Pope Julius II">Pope Julius II</a> decreed that her relics should be kept there.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Patronage">Patronage</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Saint_Anne&amp;action=edit&amp;section=17" title="Edit section: Patronage">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>The Church of Saint Anne in <a href="/wiki/Beit_Guvrin_National_Park" class="mw-redirect" title="Beit Guvrin National Park">Beit Guvrin National Park</a> was built by the <a href="/wiki/Byzantine_Greeks" title="Byzantine Greeks">Byzantines</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Crusades" title="Crusades">Crusaders</a> in the twelfth century, known in Arabic as Khirbet (lit. "ruin") Sandahanna, the mound of Maresha being called Tell Sandahanna.
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Basilique_Saint-Anne.JPG" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/48/Basilique_Saint-Anne.JPG/220px-Basilique_Saint-Anne.JPG" decoding="async" width="220" height="330" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/48/Basilique_Saint-Anne.JPG/330px-Basilique_Saint-Anne.JPG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/48/Basilique_Saint-Anne.JPG/440px-Basilique_Saint-Anne.JPG 2x" data-file-width="2575" data-file-height="3863" /></a><figcaption><a href="/wiki/Basilica_of_Sainte-Anne-de-Beaupr%C3%A9" title="Basilica of Sainte-Anne-de-Beaupré">Basilica of Sainte-Anne-de-Beaupré</a>, <a href="/wiki/Quebec" title="Quebec">Quebec</a>, Canada</figcaption></figure>
<p>Saint Anne is patroness of unmarried women, housewives, women in labor or who want to be pregnant, grandmothers, mothers and educators. She is also a patroness of horseback riders, cabinet-makers<sup id="cite_ref-crawley_16-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-crawley-16">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup> and miners. As the mother of Mary, this devotion to Saint Anne as the patron of miners arises from the medieval comparison between Mary and Christ and the precious metals silver and gold. Anne's womb was considered the source from which these precious metals were mined.<sup id="cite_ref-32" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-32">&#91;31&#93;</a></sup> 
</p><p>She is also the <a href="/wiki/Patron_saint" title="Patron saint">patron saint</a> of: <a href="/wiki/Brittany" title="Brittany">Brittany</a> (<a href="/wiki/France" title="France">France</a>), <a href="/wiki/Chinandega" title="Chinandega">Chinandega</a> (<a href="/wiki/Nicaragua" title="Nicaragua">Nicaragua</a>), the <a href="/wiki/Mi%27kmaq" title="Mi&#39;kmaq">Mi'kmaq</a> people of <a href="/wiki/Canada" title="Canada">Canada</a>, <a href="/wiki/Castelbuono" title="Castelbuono">Castelbuono</a> (Sicily), <a href="/wiki/Quebec" title="Quebec">Quebec</a> (<a href="/wiki/Canada" title="Canada">Canada</a>), <a href="/wiki/Santa_Ana,_California" title="Santa Ana, California">Santa Ana</a> (<a href="/wiki/California" title="California">California</a>), <a href="/wiki/Norwich,_Connecticut" title="Norwich, Connecticut">Norwich</a> (<a href="/wiki/Connecticut" title="Connecticut">Connecticut</a>), <a href="/wiki/Detroit,_Michigan" class="mw-redirect" title="Detroit, Michigan">Detroit</a> (<a href="/wiki/Michigan" title="Michigan">Michigan</a>),<sup id="cite_ref-33" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-33">&#91;32&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Adjuntas,_Puerto_Rico" title="Adjuntas, Puerto Rico">Adjuntas</a> (<a href="/wiki/Puerto_Rico" title="Puerto Rico">Puerto Rico</a>), <a href="/wiki/Santa_Ana,_El_Salvador" title="Santa Ana, El Salvador">Santa Ana</a> and <a href="/wiki/Jucuar%C3%A1n" title="Jucuarán">Jucuarán</a> (<a href="/wiki/El_Salvador" title="El Salvador">El Salvador</a>), <a href="/wiki/Berlin,_New_Hampshire" title="Berlin, New Hampshire">Berlin</a> (<a href="/wiki/New_Hampshire" title="New Hampshire">New Hampshire</a>), <a href="/wiki/Santa_Ana_Pueblo,_New_Mexico" title="Santa Ana Pueblo, New Mexico">Santa Ana Pueblo</a>, <a href="/wiki/Seama,_New_Mexico" title="Seama, New Mexico">Seama</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Taos,_New_Mexico" title="Taos, New Mexico">Taos</a> (<a href="/wiki/New_Mexico" title="New Mexico">New Mexico</a>), <a href="/wiki/Chiclana_de_la_Frontera" title="Chiclana de la Frontera">Chiclana de la Frontera</a>, <a href="/wiki/Marsaskala" title="Marsaskala">Marsaskala</a>, <a href="/wiki/Tudela,_Navarre" title="Tudela, Navarre">Tudela</a> and <a href="/wiki/Fasnia" title="Fasnia">Fasnia</a> (<a href="/wiki/Spain" title="Spain">Spain</a>), <a href="/wiki/Santa_Ana,_Pampanga" title="Santa Ana, Pampanga">Town of Sta Ana Province of Pampanga</a>, St. Anne in <a href="/wiki/Molo,_Iloilo_City" title="Molo, Iloilo City">Molo, Iloilo City</a>, <a href="/wiki/Hagonoy,_Bulacan" title="Hagonoy, Bulacan">Hagonoy</a>, Santa Ana, <a href="/wiki/Taguig_City" class="mw-redirect" title="Taguig City">Taguig City</a>, <a href="/wiki/Saint_Anne_Parish_and_Diocesan_Shrine_(Malicboy)" class="mw-redirect" title="Saint Anne Parish and Diocesan Shrine (Malicboy)">Saint Anne Shrine</a>, Malicboy, <a href="/wiki/Pagbilao" title="Pagbilao">Pagbilao</a>, <a href="/wiki/Quezon" title="Quezon">Quezon</a> and <a href="/wiki/Malinao,_Albay" title="Malinao, Albay">Malinao, Albay</a> (<a href="/wiki/Philippines" title="Philippines">Philippines</a>), <a href="/wiki/Santana_(district_of_S%C3%A3o_Paulo)" title="Santana (district of São Paulo)">Santana</a> (<a href="/wiki/Brazil" title="Brazil">Brazil</a>), <a href="/wiki/Saint_Anne,_Illinois" class="mw-redirect" title="Saint Anne, Illinois">Saint Anne</a> (<a href="/wiki/Illinois" title="Illinois">Illinois</a>), Sainte Anne Island, <a href="/wiki/Baie_Sainte_Anne" class="mw-redirect" title="Baie Sainte Anne">Baie Sainte Anne</a> and <a href="/wiki/Praslin_Island" class="mw-redirect" title="Praslin Island">Praslin Island</a> (<a href="/wiki/Seychelles" title="Seychelles">Seychelles</a>), <a href="/wiki/Bukit_Mertajam" title="Bukit Mertajam">Bukit Mertajam</a> and <a href="/wiki/Port_Klang" title="Port Klang">Port Klang</a> (<a href="/wiki/Malaysia" title="Malaysia">Malaysia</a>), Kľúčové (<a href="/wiki/Slovakia" title="Slovakia">Slovakia</a>) and <a href="/wiki/South_Vietnam" title="South Vietnam">South Vietnam</a>. The <a href="/wiki/Parish_church" title="Parish church">parish church</a> of <a href="/wiki/Vatican_City" title="Vatican City">Vatican City</a> is <a href="/wiki/Sant%27Anna_dei_Palafrenieri" title="Sant&#39;Anna dei Palafrenieri">Sant'Anna dei Palafrenieri</a>. There is a shrine dedicated to Saint Anne in the Woods in <a href="/wiki/Bristol" title="Bristol">Bristol</a>, United Kingdom.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="In_art">In art</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Saint_Anne&amp;action=edit&amp;section=18" title="Edit section: In art">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Christ_in_the_House_of_His_Parents"><i>Christ in the House of His Parents</i></span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Saint_Anne&amp;action=edit&amp;section=19" title="Edit section: Christ in the House of His Parents">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:John_Everett_Millais_-_Christ_in_the_House_of_His_Parents_(%60The_Carpenter%27s_Shop%27)_-_Google_Art_Project.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7b/John_Everett_Millais_-_Christ_in_the_House_of_His_Parents_%28%60The_Carpenter%27s_Shop%27%29_-_Google_Art_Project.jpg/220px-John_Everett_Millais_-_Christ_in_the_House_of_His_Parents_%28%60The_Carpenter%27s_Shop%27%29_-_Google_Art_Project.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="134" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7b/John_Everett_Millais_-_Christ_in_the_House_of_His_Parents_%28%60The_Carpenter%27s_Shop%27%29_-_Google_Art_Project.jpg/330px-John_Everett_Millais_-_Christ_in_the_House_of_His_Parents_%28%60The_Carpenter%27s_Shop%27%29_-_Google_Art_Project.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7b/John_Everett_Millais_-_Christ_in_the_House_of_His_Parents_%28%60The_Carpenter%27s_Shop%27%29_-_Google_Art_Project.jpg/440px-John_Everett_Millais_-_Christ_in_the_House_of_His_Parents_%28%60The_Carpenter%27s_Shop%27%29_-_Google_Art_Project.jpg 2x" data-file-width="4500" data-file-height="2731" /></a><figcaption><i><a href="/wiki/Christ_in_the_House_of_His_Parents" title="Christ in the House of His Parents">Christ in the House of His Parents</a></i> by <a href="/wiki/John_Everett_Millais" title="John Everett Millais">John Everett Millais</a>, 1849–50</figcaption></figure>
<p>In <a href="/wiki/John_Everett_Millais" title="John Everett Millais">John Everett Millais</a>'s 1849–50 work, <i><a href="/wiki/Christ_in_the_House_of_His_Parents" title="Christ in the House of His Parents">Christ in the House of His Parents</a></i>,
Anne is shown in her son-in-law <a href="/wiki/Saint_Joseph" title="Saint Joseph">Joseph's</a> carpentry shop caring for a young Jesus who had cut his hand on a nail. She joins her daughter <a href="/wiki/Mary,_Mother_of_Jesus" class="mw-redirect" title="Mary, Mother of Jesus">Mary</a>, Joseph, and a young boy who will later become known as <a href="/wiki/John_the_Baptist" title="John the Baptist">John the Baptist</a> in caring for the injured hand of Jesus.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Iconography">Iconography</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Saint_Anne&amp;action=edit&amp;section=20" title="Edit section: Iconography">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>The subject of Joachim and Anne <i><a href="/wiki/Joachim_and_Anne_Meeting_at_the_Golden_Gate" title="Joachim and Anne Meeting at the Golden Gate">The Meeting at the Golden Gate</a></i> was a regular component of artistic cycles of the <a href="/wiki/Life_of_the_Virgin" title="Life of the Virgin">Life of the Virgin</a>. The couple meet at the <a href="/wiki/Golden_Gate_(Jerusalem)" title="Golden Gate (Jerusalem)">Golden Gate</a> of <a href="/wiki/Jerusalem" title="Jerusalem">Jerusalem</a> and embrace. They are aware of Anne's pregnancy, of which they have been separately informed by an archangel. This moment stood for the conception of Mary, and the feast was celebrated on the same day as the <a href="/wiki/Immaculate_Conception" title="Immaculate Conception">Immaculate Conception</a>. Art works representing the Golden Gate and the events leading up to it were influenced by the narrative in the widely read <i>Golden Legend</i> of Jacobus de Voragine. The <a href="/wiki/Nativity_of_Mary" title="Nativity of Mary">Birth of Mary</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Presentation_of_Mary" title="Presentation of Mary">Presentation of Mary</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Marriage_of_the_Virgin" title="Marriage of the Virgin">Marriage of the Virgin</a> were usual components of cycles of the Life of the Virgin in which Anne is normally shown here.
</p><p>Her emblem is a door.<sup id="cite_ref-crawley_16-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-crawley-16">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup> She is often portrayed wearing red and green, representing love and life.<sup id="cite_ref-Fongemie_3-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Fongemie-3">&#91;3&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Anne is never shown as present at the <a href="/wiki/Nativity_of_Jesus_in_art" title="Nativity of Jesus in art">Nativity of Christ</a>, but is frequently shown with the infant Christ in various subjects. She is sometimes believed to be depicted in scenes of the <a href="/wiki/Presentation_of_Jesus_at_the_Temple" class="mw-redirect" title="Presentation of Jesus at the Temple">Presentation of Jesus at the Temple</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Circumcision_of_Christ" class="mw-redirect" title="Circumcision of Christ">Circumcision of Christ</a>, but in the former case, this likely reflects a misidentification through confusion with <a href="/wiki/Anna_the_Prophetess" title="Anna the Prophetess">Anna the Prophetess</a>. There was a tradition that Anne went (separately) to Egypt and rejoined the <a href="/wiki/Holy_Family" title="Holy Family">Holy Family</a> after their <a href="/wiki/Flight_to_Egypt" class="mw-redirect" title="Flight to Egypt">Flight to Egypt</a>. Anne is not seen with the adult Christ, so was regarded as having died during the youth of Jesus.<sup id="cite_ref-34" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-34">&#91;33&#93;</a></sup> Anne is also shown as the matriarch of the <a href="/wiki/Holy_Kinship" title="Holy Kinship">Holy Kinship</a>, the extended family of Jesus, a popular subject in late medieval Germany; some versions of these pictorial and sculptural depictions include <a href="/wiki/Emerentia" title="Emerentia">Emerentia</a> who was reputed in the fifteenth century to be Anne's mother. In modern devotions, Anne and her husband are invoked for protection for the unborn.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Virgin_and_Child_with_Saint_Anne">Virgin and Child with Saint Anne</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Saint_Anne&amp;action=edit&amp;section=21" title="Edit section: Virgin and Child with Saint Anne">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>The role of the Messiah's grandparents in salvation history was commonly depicted in early medieval devotional art in a vertical double-Madonna arrangement known as the <a href="/wiki/Virgin_and_Child_with_Saint_Anne" title="Virgin and Child with Saint Anne">Virgin and Child with Saint Anne</a>. Another typical subject has Anne teaching the Virgin Mary the Scriptures (see gallery below).
</p>

<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Music">Music</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Saint_Anne&amp;action=edit&amp;section=23" title="Edit section: Music">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<ul><li><a href="/wiki/Marc-Antoine_Charpentier" title="Marc-Antoine Charpentier">Marc-Antoine Charpentier</a> composed 2 <i>motets&#160;:</i>
<ul><li><i>Pour Ste Anne,</i> H.315, for 2 voices and continuo (around 1675)</li>
<li><i>Canticum Annae,</i> H.325, for 3 voices, 2 treble instruments, and continuo (around 1680).</li></ul></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Johann_Sebastian_Bach" title="Johann Sebastian Bach">Johann Sebastian Bach</a> composed a prelude and fugue&#160;:
<ul><li><a href="/wiki/Clavier-%C3%9Cbung_III#Prelude_and_fugue_BWV_552" title="Clavier-Übung III">
Prelude and Fugue in E-Flat Major, BWV 552</a> (published 1739)</li></ul></li></ul>
